(vi) Cessation of Hyperinflationary Accounting
(a) Criteria and Overview of Cessation:
In prior reporting periods, including the financial year ended December 31, 2025, the Group designated its operating subsidiaries in Ghana and Sierra Leone as hyperinflationary
economies in accordance with IAS 29: Financial Reporting in Hyperinflationary Economies. This designation was based on quantitative factors - principally where the three-year
cumulative inflation rate approached or exceeded 100% - alongside supporting qualitative indicators of the macroeconomic environment.
During the first half of 2026, both economies experienced sustained economic stabilisation, marked by sharp, consistent declines in annual inflation rates and the strengthening of
their respective domestic currencies. Consequently, based on consensus determinations from global accounting bodies and the International Practices Task Force (IPTF), Ghana
and Sierra Leone are no longer regarded as hyperinflationary economies for financial reporting purposes effective from the current interim reporting period ended June 30, 2026.
(b) Price Indices Summary
The core consumer price indices (CPI) used to determine this transition, obtained from the International Monetary Fund (IMF) and respective local national statistics bureaus, are

excluded from those included in determining effective interest rates on those carried at amortized cost
[4]Commissions on transactional service represents one-off charges on Banking transactions such as cheque book issuance,
Bank statement fees, cash management, stopped cheques and other transactional related income.

(d) Reconciliation of effective tax rate
The tax on the Group’s profit before tax differs from the theoretical amount that would arise using the tax rate applicable to
profits of the Bank (Parent). The reconciliation of amount reported as tax expense in the statement of comprehensive income
to the income tax using the domestic corporation tax rate is presented below:

Companies Income Tax:
In accordance with the provisions of the Nigeria Tax Act (NTA), 2025, companies with an annual aggregate turnover of ₦50
billion and above are subject to Companies Income Tax (CIT) at a rate of 30% on their taxable profits.
The NTA also introduced a Top-up Tax regime applicable to multinational enterprise groups with consolidated annual revenues
of at least EUR 750 million. The objective of this regime is to ensure that such groups are subject to a minimum Effective Tax Rate
(ETR) of 15%.
Where the ETR of a constituent entity falls below the prescribed minimum threshold, a Top-up Tax becomes payable to bridge
the gap and ensure that the minimum ETR of 15% is achieved. The tax is computed based on the applicable net income of the
affected entities.
During the 2025 financial year, UBA Plc recognized a Top-up Tax exposure of approximately ₦29 billion arising from certain
subsidiaries whose ETRs were below the minimum 15% threshold. This liability has been provided for in the Bank's 2026 half-year
financial statements, following clarification of the applicable compliance requirements and the resolution of uncertainties that
previously existed regarding whether the 2025 filing obligations would be administered under the provisions of the Companies
Income Tax Act (CITA) or the Nigeria Tax Act (NTA).
For the six-month period ended 30 June 2026, no additional Top-up Tax charge was recognized by the Bank.
i. Development Levy:
The Nigeria Tax Act (NTA), 2025 introduced the Development Levy as a consolidated levy replacing the former Tertiary
Education Tax (Education Tax), NITDA Levy, NASENI Levy, and Police Trust Fund (PTF) Levy.
Under the Act, companies with assessable profits are required to pay a Development Levy at the rate of 4% of assessable profit,
after applicable tax adjustments.
For the six-month period ended 30 June 2026, UBA Plc recorded no Development Levy liability, as the Bank had no assessable
profit subject to the levy during the period.
Accordingly, the Development Levy charge for the half year was Nil.
ii. Other Levies:
The Nigeria Police Trust Fund levy, Education Tax, National Agency for Science and Engineering Infrastructure (NASENI) levy
and NASENI were applicable under CITA but has now been abolished under the NTA with effect from 2026 FY.

( c) Impairment testing for cash-generating units containing Goodwill
For the purpose of impairment testing, goodwill acquired through business combinations is allocated to cash generating units (CGUs) as the goodwill is monitored at the level of the individual
cash generating units. UBA Benin, UBA Zambia, and UBA UK Limited have been identified as individual cash generating units. UBA Benin and UBA Zambia operate under Rest of Africa
geagraphic segments while UBA UK Limited operates under Rest of the World geographic segment. The recoverable amounts of the CGUs have been determined based on value-in-use
calculations; using cash flow projections based on financial forecasts covering a period of five years. Cash flows beyond the five-year period are extrapolated using estimated economic
The following table sets out the key assumptions used in the value-in-use calculations:
UBA Benin UBA UK Limited UBA Zambia
Jun. 2026 Dec. 2025 Jun. 2026 Dec. 2025 Jun. 2026 Dec. 2025
Gross earnings ( % annual growth rate) 20.9 43.5 26.5 46.3 17 5 3
Deposits (% annual growth rate) 34.7 31.5 74.9 55.8 50.4 3 8.5
Loans and advances (% annual growth rate) 32.3 39.8 (1.1) (14.8) 162 1 76
Operating expenses (% annual growth rate) 11.6 39.7 34.5 62.0 23 3 8
Terminal growth rate (%) 7.0 7.0 2.0 1.3 6 6
Discount rate (pre-tax) (%) 15.6 11.9 6.0 7.9 19.2 2 6
The values assigned to each of the above key assumptions were determined as follows:
Assumption Approach used in determining values
Gross earnings Thisistheaverageannualgrowthrateoverthefive-yearperiod.Basedonpastperformance,expectationsofmarket
development and the expected positive impact of deposits and loan growth in the forecast period.
Deposits Thisistheaverageannualgrowthrateoverthefive-yearperiod.Depositshavebeendeterminedtobethekeyvaluedriver
fortheCGUs.ProjecteddepositsgrowthisbasedonpastperformanceoftheCGUsaswellasmanagement'splansto
expand the businesses and deepen customer base.
Loans and advances Thisistheaverageannualgrowthrateoverthefiveyearperiod.Itisbasedpartlyonpastperformancebutlargelyonthe
expected positive impact of the forecasted growth in deposits.
Operating expenses Thisistheaverageannualgrowthrateoverthefiveyearperiod.Itisbasedonthecurrentstructureofbusinessofthe
respectiveCGUs,adjustingforexpectedinflationaryincreasesbutnotreflectinganyfuturerestructuringsorcostsaving
measures.
Terminal growth rate Thisistheaveragegrowthrateusedtoextrapolatecashflowsbeyondthefive-yearperiod.Basedonestimatedeconomic
growth rates for the respective CGUs.
Discount rate Thediscountratewasapre-taxmeasurebasedonthelongesttenuredgovernmentbondissuedbythegovernmentsin
BeninandUnitedKingdomrespectivelyadjustedforariskpremiumtoreflectboththeincreasedriskofinvestinginequities
and generally and the systematic risk of the specific CGU.

37.1 This represents on-lending facilities provided by the Central Bank of Nigeria (CBN):
(a) N13.61 billion of this facility represents the outstanding balance on the Commercial Agriculture Credit Scheme granted to the Bank for the sole purpose of granting loans, at subsidised rates, to
the agricultural sector. Interest on the facility cannot exceed 9% per annum inclusive of all charges and is to be shared between the Bank and CBN at 6% and 3% respectively. The Bank is the
primary obligor to CBN and assumes the risk of default.
(b) N3.80 billion of this facility represents the outstanding balance on the loan granted by the Central Bank of Nigeria with respect to Real Sector Support Facility (RSSF) initiative to support the
Federal Government’s Special Fertilizer Intervention programme. The Central Bank shall lend to the Bank at 3% while the Bank shall on-lend to the customer at a maximum interest rate of 6% per
annum, all charges inclusive. The 3% interest shall be remitted to CBN on a quarterly basis. The principal is repayable quarterly (after a one year moratorium) and the tenor of the facility is 7
years.
37.2 This represents an intervention credit granted to the Bank by the Bank of Industry (BOI) for the purpose of refinancing/restructuring existing loans to Small and Medium Scale Enterprises (SMEs),
manufacturing companies and companies in the power and aviation industries. The maximum tenor of term loans under the programme is 15 years while the tenor for working capital is one
year, renewable annually subject to a maximum tenor of five years. A management fee of 1% per annum, deductible at source in the first year and quarterly in arrears thereafter, is paid by the
Bank under the intervention programme and the Bank is under obligation to on-lend to customers at an all-interest rate of 5% per annum. The Bank is the primary obligor to CBN/BOI and
assumes the risk of default.
37.3 This represents the amortised cost of the Eurobond issued by the Bank in November, 2021. The $300million Notes issued by the Bank on November 19 2021 is for a tenor of 5 years with interest
rate(coupon) of 6.75% p.a, payable semi-annually with bullet repayment of the Principal sum at maturity. The maturity date of the Eurobond is November 19, 2026.
37.4 This represents the amount granted under a $85 million trade loan facility granted by Proparco in April 2020. The facility is for a tenor of seven (7) years and Interest rate is six (6) months USD
LIBOR (amended to six months USD SOFR with effect from 30th June 2023) plus 320 basis points. The interest repayments are payable semi-annually while the principal repayment commenced
on a semi-annual basis following the 2 year grace period. Outstanding balance on the facility is $8.63 million and the facility matures in October 2026.
37.5 This represents the amount granted under a $300million trade finance loan facility granted by Afrexim Bank in November 2025 with a tenor of twelve(12) months. The Interest rate is twelve (12)
months USD LIBOR + 2.0%. The interest repayment and the principal repayment will be made at the end of the tenor.
37.6 The Bank of Zambia granted UBA Zambia K57.558 million and K125.216 million facilities under the Targeted Medium-Term Refinancing Facility (TMTRF) and under the Stability and Resilience
Facility (SRF), respectively. The medium-term facility was availed on 13 April 2022 at an interest rate of 9% per annum and maturing on 12 April 2027, to provide facilities to customers whose
businesses and operations were adversely affected by the COVID-19 pandemic.
The Stability and Resilience Facility (SRF) was availed on 3 June 2025 at an interest rate of 13.5% per annum and maturing on 3 June 2028 to provide loan facilities to customers affected by the
energy crisis in Zambia. The outstanding balance on these facilities was K100 million (N13.91bn).

(iii)Loan commitments
Loan commitments are irrevocable commitments to provide credits under pre-specified terms and conditions. The Group's loan commitments are usually conditioned on the
maintenance of a satisfactory financial standing by the customer and absence of defaults on other covenants. At the reporting date, the Group had loan commitments amounting
to N328.99 billion (2025: N151.79 billion); Bank: N317.55 billion (2025: N94.19 billion) in respect of various loan contracts.

43 IAS 29 - Financial Reporting in Hyperinflationary Economies
Effective from January 1, 2026, the Group has discontinued the application of hyperinflationary accounting for its subsidiaries, United Bank for Africa (Ghana) Limited and United Bank
for Africa (Sierra Leone) Limited. The financial statements of these subsidiaries for the six months ended June 30, 2026, have been prepared under the standard historical cost
convention. In accordance with the transition requirements of IAS 29:
• The carrying amounts of non-monetary assets, non-monetary liabilities, and equity components, as restated to the last applicable reporting date, December 31, 2025, have been
treated as the initial historical cost baseline for the HY2026 reporting period.
• Depreciation, amortisation, and asset consumption post-January 1, 2026, are calculated using these newly established historical cost baselines rather than further indexation.
• Transactions occurring between January 1, 2026, and June 30, 2026, are translated using regular transaction-date spot exchange rates. The Group no longer recognises a net
monetary gain or loss in the Consolidated Statement of Profit or Loss for these two subsidiaries.

46 Events after the reporting date
There were no other events after the reporting date that could have had a material effect on the financial condition of the Group and the Bank as at 30 June 2026 and the profit
and other comprehensive income for the period ended which have not been adjusted or disclosed.
However, the tenure of the Chairman of the Board of Directors Mr Tony O. Elumelu, CFR came to an end on August 21,2026. Following the expiration of his tenure, the Board of
Directors appointed Mr. Emmanuel Nnorom as Chairman of the Board, effective immediately. The change did not have any financial impact on the financial statements for the
period ended 30 June 2026.
